# The house standard against outside standards

Generated by `scripts/standards.mjs`. Do not edit; change `drills/standards.yaml` or a framework's `basis` / `std`.

Every objective has a house code (`HT-2.2.A`) that is ours. An objective is **anchored** when it, its topic or its unit cites an item of an outside standard, and **house only** when nothing outside asks for it yet.

### Notes

- **Abstract Algebra**: Anchored on the Cambridge Groups and Groups, Rings and Modules schedules (read through an extraction). Group theory is the weight, since it is what chemistry uses; rings stop at unique factorisation, and modules appear only as the classification of finite abelian groups. Fields and Galois theory are not built.
- **Financial Accounting (CPA FAR)**: Units are the FAR blueprint's three areas and topics are its groups; the objectives are ours, written against the group titles (the representative tasks were not read).
- **AI Basics**: Units follow the ten modules of Harper Carroll's free Academy course. Only her module titles were readable, so the objectives under them are a house list.
- **AI Safety**: No accredited standard exists; course syllabi and lab frameworks are the closest thing.
- **Analytical Chemistry**: Primary since Nov 2026: the ACS analytical content map, read in full; every articulation is answered (unit 6 was built for the ones nothing else reached). Earlier topics were named by IChO Appendix C, GRE Chemistry and MCAT 5C. Objectives and their order are ours.
- **Anatomy and Physiology**: Anchored on the HAPS Anatomy and Physiology Learning Outcomes (2019), read through its module and topic headings only. Cell biology (module C) and heredity (module S) are left to the Biology framework. Clinical judgment is in NCLEX and INBDE; this is the normal structure and function they assume.
- **Anchor Quantities**: Values from CODATA 2022, standard atomic weights and 21 CFR 101.9. Which values to carry is a house list for units 1–4 and the SnapShot Key Numbers in Biology sheet for units 5–6. No chemistry standard lists these values; the ACS Physical Chemistry content map (read) asks for the mole-scale and Boltzmann reasoning they serve, and AQ objectives cite those articulations.
- **Basketball**: Introductory level read in full, higher levels in overview only.
- **Biochemistry**: Concepts and statements from ASBMB (core concepts paper and exam objectives); the objective list is ours.
- **Biology (AP)**: Topics cite the 2025 AP Biology CED learning objectives (all 97 read); objectives keep their 2019 codes, read through a publisher's correlation table.
- **Biosecurity**: Units follow the six units of BlueDot Impact's Biosecurity course (outline read Nov 2026); laboratory biosafety follows the WHO Laboratory Biosafety Manual's risk-based structure and the treaty items follow the BWC's articles. No curriculum standard for biosecurity exists; the objectives are a house list.
- **Chemistry Olympiad**: Topics from the USNCO bands (read in part) and IChO Appendix C (read in full), at national-exam depth; the items are a house list.
- **Chemistry (AP)**: Every topic in units 1–9 cites its College Board learning objectives (92 of 92, read Nov 2026). Objectives past Unit 2 were first written from topic titles; check them against the LO text when revising.
- **Discrete Mathematics**: Anchored on the MAA CUPM 2015 report's discrete mathematics, enumerative combinatorics and graph theory syllabi (goals and syllabi read through an extraction). Computer Science keeps the algorithmic applications; this framework is the mathematics under them.
- **Earth and Space Science**: Units follow the NGSS HS ESS disciplinary core ideas (ESS1 Earth's place in the universe, ESS2 Earth's systems, ESS3 Earth and human activity), read in part. Objectives and their order are ours.
- **Electric Circuits and Signals**: Anchored on the NCEES FE Electrical and Computer specification, sections 6-10 and 12, read to their lettered lines. Electromagnetics (section 11) is left to Physics and is not cited; communications, networks, digital and computer systems and software are in Computer Science or not built. Circuits are described in words because no schematic figure type exists yet.
- **Electric Double Layers**: A course textbook, not a curriculum standard: the units follow its chapters and the objectives are ours. Colloids and DLVO at the undergraduate level live in Polymers and Materials unit 3 (Israelachvili).
- **Electronic Structure**: No accredited standard exists at this level. The Psi4NumPy modules are the closest published curriculum and the MolSSI lesson fixes the practical units; Szabo & Ostlund is a theory reference only. Objectives are ours.
- **Applied Engineering Mathematics**: UCI CBE 200 publishes no syllabus. The undergraduate part is anchored to fe-chemical; series, Sturm–Liouville, PDEs and transforms are past any exam spec and stand on the book.
- **Fluid Mechanics**: Topics from FE Chemical area 6, read in full; units 7–10 extend past it on the WeBWorK OPL fluid-mechanics sections (read in full). Objectives and their order are ours; statics is assumed by the spec rather than listed.
- **Geometry**: Anchored on the Cambridge Part IB Geometry schedule (read through an extraction). The three constant-curvature geometries side by side. Axiomatic Euclidean geometry, projective geometry and the differential geometry of general surfaces are not built; K-12 geometry lives in the school mathematics frameworks.
- **Graduate Chemical Engineering Core**: No body publishes an itemised graduate chemical engineering content standard, so the primary basis is our own crosswalk of fifteen departments' published core-course requirements, each read in full. IChemE Level F carries the level this framework sits at: its outcomes are the same principles on more complex problems with the limits of the assumptions interrogated. UCI's catalogue descriptions, which this framework used to rest on alone, are now one department among the fifteen. PE Chemical is the nearest published exam and stops below this level, and FE Chemical closes the undergraduate layer that several departments' qualifying exams actually test.
- **Heat Transfer**: Also crosswalked to the FE Mechanical spec and UCI MAE 120.
- **INBDE (Dentistry)**: Units are the Domain of Dentistry's three clinical sections plus Foundation Knowledge area 3 (materials); objectives cite the clinical content area or FK they serve and are ours.
- **Advanced Inorganic Chemistry (Cotton & Wilkinson)**: Since Nov 2026 the primary basis is the ACS inorganic content map (read in full); every articulation is answered (scripts/accm_maps.py), unit 15 holding the principles the chapter units did not reach. Units follow the chapters of Cotton & Wilkinson 6e (table of contents read); the objectives are a house list on those chapters.
- **Inorganic Chemistry**: Units 1, 2 and 4 follow IChO Appendix D's factual-knowledge list (read in full); coordination and solids follow its preparation-only topics and the GRE inorganic area. Objectives and their order are ours.
- **Linear Algebra**: Anchored on the MAA CUPM 2023 linear algebra course report (read in full): every essential and optional core topic is cited by objectives here. Units keep Lay's order; determinants and LU, which the 2023 list leaves out, rest on Lay and the 2015 guide.
- **Managerial Accounting**: Anchored on the IMA CMA Content Specification Outlines (2024): Part 1 planning and budgeting, performance management and cost management, and Part 2 decision analysis and capital investment, read to the lettered lines of the outline. Financial reporting, internal controls, analytics, corporate finance, risk and ethics sections of the CMA are not built here.
- **Mass Transfer**: PE Chemical names mass transfer as one area with two lines (6.A fundamentals, 6.B applications). No published standard lists graduate mass transfer; the objectives are a house list on Bird, Stewart & Lightfoot's mass-transport chapters.
- **Material and Energy Balances**: Topics from FE Chemical area 8, read in full. Objectives and their order are ours and follow the usual textbook sequence; unit 1 is assumed by the spec rather than listed.
- **Polymers and Materials**: Polymer topics from IChO Appendix C (c22, a5), IB S2.4 and FE Chemical 4.D; a house list on those items.
- **Mathematics K–8 (Common Core)**: Codes and wording checked against a state reprint; the CCSS site was blocked.
- **Mental Math**: Topic list and order from the UIL high-school chart. The short-cut techniques and the estimation topic are a house list. Unit 6 goes past the chart: the cover-up items follow an MIT 18.03 course note and the one-a-minute integration pace follows the MIT Integration Bee qualifier; which results belong in unit 6 is a house list. Pace comes from the UIL rules (7.5 s a problem) and the English multiplication tables check (6 s a fact).
- **Microbiology**: Anchored on the ASM Curriculum Guidelines for Undergraduate Microbiology (2024), read through an extraction of its concept areas, fundamental statements and skills. A general course: structure, growth, genetics, ecology, control and laboratory counting. Organism-by-organism medical microbiology is not built; host defence is in Anatomy and Physiology unit 9.
- **Microeconomics**: Anchored on the AP Microeconomics Course and Exam Description (2022), read through its unit and topic list. Introductory level with linear curves. Indifference curves, isoquants, Cournot and Bertrand models and general equilibrium belong to an intermediate course and are not built; macroeconomics has no framework yet.
- **Molecular Simulation**: No accredited standard exists for this subject. Kevin's own group's orientation repository is the closest thing to a curriculum for the work he will actually do, and it was read in full, so it is primary; the best-practice papers and the alchemistry wiki fix the parts it assumes.
- **NCLEX-RN**: Units are the test plan's eight Client Needs subcategories (with their weights); topics group its related-content items; the objectives are ours.
- **NGSS High School Science**: One objective per high-school performance expectation. The PE statements were read; the evidence statements were not. Drills for PEs taught elsewhere are drawn from the subject frameworks; the engineering and design PEs have their own items.
- **Nuclear Science and Reactor Theory**: Reactor-theory units follow the NRC Generic Fundamentals Examination topic list (read in part). Nuclear physics and shielding follow the DOE fundamentals handbook's first module (read in part). Health-physics standards for the radiation-protection unit have not been looked at.
- **Number Theory**: Anchored on the Cambridge Numbers and Sets and Number Theory schedules (read through an extraction). The Jacobi symbol, binary quadratic forms and factorisation methods (T4, T5, T9) are not drilled in draft 1.
- **Numerical Methods**: FE Chemical names numerical methods as one item (1.D) and the WeBWorK OPL names a few methods; no published standard lists a numerical-methods course, so the objectives are a house list on Chapra & Canale's part order.
- **Organic Reactions: Predict the Product**: Since Nov 2026 the primary basis is the ACS organic content map (read in full); every articulation is answered (scripts/accm_maps.py), with units 9 (physical organic, practice) and 10 (organometallics) added for those nothing reached. Families follow the reaction chapters of Bruice's Organic Chemistry (a textbook, not a standard; chapter titles 2-17 from memory). The reactions and substrates in each are a house list. Every product is mass-balanced against its reactant by the generator; regiochemistry and stereochemistry were checked by eye.
- **Particle Physics**: Objectives follow AQA A-level Physics section 3.2 (Particles and radiation), read in this session. The Lorentz-factor items go beyond it and rest on a university textbook chapter.
- **The Periodic Table from Memory**: Content from the IUPAC table (release note read; element list cross-checked in the verifier). Which parts to know by heart is a house decision from Kevin's organometallics course.
- **Pharmacology**: Units 1-3 are anchored on the IUPHAR core concepts of pharmacology (24 concepts read through an extraction). Units 4-8, the drug classes, are a house list in textbook order because the ASPET Pharmacology Learning Objectives (2026) could not be fetched; rebase them when that document is read. Class, mechanism and signature adverse effect only: no dosing regimens, and nothing here is clinical advice.
- **Physical Chemistry**: Anchored on the ACS Physical Chemistry content map (read in full): objectives cite its subdisciplinary articulations, through scripts/accm_pchem.py. Units keep Engel & Reid's order; the map's articulations no objective answers are listed in docs/coverage/standards.md.
- **Physics (AP)**: Objective codes read for a few units only.
- **Process Design, Economics and Safety**: Topics from FE Chemical areas 11, 13, 14, 15, 16 and 17 and PE Chemical area 7 (read in full); objectives and their order are ours. The professional-practice unit rests on the NSPE Code of Ethics (read in full) and the safety-management unit on 29 CFR 1910.119 (paragraph titles read). Particle technology has no published curriculum of its own, so that unit is a house list on the FE spec items.
- **Computational Protein Design**: No accredited standard exists. The notebook workshops are the closest published curriculum; the energy-function paper, the deep-learning workshops and the review fix the content of the later units. Objectives are ours.
- **Chemical Reaction Engineering**: Topics from FE Chemical area 12, read in full. Objectives and their order are ours. Units 5–8 and the later letters of units 1–4 go beyond the FE spec on Fogler 5e's chapter order (a house list); the gaps are in the file header.
- **Real Analysis**: Anchored on the Cambridge Analysis I schedule (read through an extraction). Uniform convergence, metric spaces and the multivariable theory (Cambridge Analysis II) are not built; Topology takes up compactness and connectedness.
- **Separations**: Topics from FE Chemical area 10 and PE Chemical 6.B (both read in full); objectives and their order are ours.
- **Speaking and Articulation**: A scoring rubric plus Toastmasters' competency list; unit order is ours.
- **SQL**: Units 1–7 follow the sixteen topic groups of Oracle's 1Z0-071 exam, read through a restatement rather than Oracle's page. Unit 8 is a house list: no standard for analytic SQL was found.
- **Statistics**: Units 1-5 follow the AP Statistics CED effective fall 2026; all 150 of its learning objectives were read and each is cited by an objective here. Objective wording and grouping are ours.
- **Stochastic Processes**: Anchored on the Cambridge Markov Chains schedule (read through an extraction), with the Poisson process and the M/M/1 queue from Applied Probability. Renewal theory, general continuous-time chains, martingales and Brownian motion are not built.
- **Structures from Memory**: Units follow the chapter list of Bruice's Organic Chemistry (a textbook, not a standard). The amino acids follow IUPAC-IUB 1983 Table 1. Which compounds to carry in each unit is a house list; the MCAT content outline names the same families and has not been opened.
- **Swimming**: The World Aquatics rules (read in full) say what is legal. What to learn, and in what order, now rests on USA Swimming's Foundations of Coaching (2007; chapters 5 and 6 read, the rest by title) and the ASCA Level 1-3 school outlines (titles only). Research papers named in an objective's own essential knowledge are its source for that claim. Unit order is ours.
- **Thermodynamics and Statistical Mechanics**: UCI CBE 240 publishes no syllabus. Classical units are anchored to fe-chemical; the statistical-mechanics units stand on the books.
- **Topology**: Anchored on the Cambridge Topological Spaces schedule (read through an extraction). Point-set topology to compactness, then surfaces by Euler characteristic. The fundamental group, homology and knot theory are not built.
- **Wushu**: Competition rules and biomechanics papers; unit order is ours.
